## 0.27.36 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 1 item 2 of "innovative feats various angles": multi-hop blast-radius graph traversal.** New `internal/insights.BlastRadius(graph, root, maxHops)`: plain breadth-first search over the existing `DependencyGraph`'s directed edges, deduping cycles and parallel edges naturally via a `visited` map. Default `maxHops` is 3, clamped to a hard ceiling of 6 — the same "beyond a handful of hops the graph degenerates toward the whole cluster" heuristic `internal/insights.Exposure` already applies at exactly 6 external dependencies. A `truncated` flag distinguishes "the graph actually ends here" from "the hop ceiling cut this off, there's more" by checking whether the final frontier still has edges to unvisited nodes.
  - **Non-negotiable wording requirement, enforced as a fixed constant, not left to callers to phrase correctly each time**: every response carries `insights.BlastRadiusCaveat` verbatim, making explicit that this is *observed traffic reachability*, never a policy allow/deny determination — a node with no further edges may still be permitted to reach destinations Netra simply hasn't seen traffic for in this window. The same wording is repeated in the new `docs/blast-radius.md` (modeled on `docs/icmp-diagnostics.md`'s "Evidence boundaries" structure) and the web UI card.
  - New `GET /api/v1/insights/blast-radius?root=&hops=` — 400 on a missing or unknown `root` (not a silently-empty graph, matching this project's existing "don't let empty read as safe" convention), requests the underlying graph at the same 5000-edge ceiling `insights/exposure` and `insights/recommendations` already use rather than the UI's smaller default, since a partial graph would understate the true radius. `hops` outside [1, 6] is silently clamped, matching how `insights/dependencies`' own `limit` parameter is handled.
  - New `netractl insights blast-radius <root> [hops]`, new `netra_insights_blast_radius` MCP read tool (tool counts bumped 98→99 total, 48→49 read tools, across `README.md`, `website/src/components/FeatureHighlights/index.tsx`, `docs/mcp-integration.md`).
  - New BLAST RADIUS card on `web/src/pages/Insights.tsx`: a root-node picker sourced from the already-loaded dependency graph, a hops selector (1-6), and a trace button — on-demand rather than polled, since tracing from an arbitrary root isn't a fixed dashboard metric. Renders the caveat text inline and a truncation warning when the hop ceiling was hit.
  - Tests (`internal/insights/blastradius_test.go`): default and ceiling-clamped hop counts, truncation reported correctly at the hop boundary vs. a fully-explored graph reporting none, an unknown root producing an error, cycles resolving without duplication or infinite loop, and parallel edges deduplicating to one.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, `helm lint`, web `tsc -b`/`vitest run` (78/78)/`build`. Pure userland graph traversal over data the existing dependency-graph endpoint already computes — no kernel/BPF verification gate applies.

## 0.27.35 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 1 item 1 of "innovative feats various angles": finish wiring capability-drift alerts.** First item of a new, deliberately non-kernel plan (AI/UX/security angles) — every item in this plan lives in `internal/*`/`cmd/*`/`web/*`, zero `bpf/netra_tc.c` changes.
  - `models.CapChangeEvent` and `internal/agent/ownership_linux.go`'s `watchCapChanges` already computed capability drift correctly, but nothing downstream ever consumed it. Verified a real, independent bug while wiring the fix: `CapChangeEvent` had no `CgroupID` field, and `Namespace`/`Pod` were always empty — the code's own comment claimed they were "filled elsewhere," but there was no elsewhere. Fixed by threading `CgroupID` through from the same `tcpHealth` snapshot `watchCapChanges`' PIDs are already sourced from (every PID it considers owns a Netra-tracked TCP socket by construction, so no second lookup mechanism was needed), then enriching `Namespace`/`Pod`/`WorkloadKind`/`WorkloadName` via the existing `a.workloadIdentity`.
  - New `internal/capdrift` package (`Build(agents, topN) models.CapDriftResponse`, mirroring `internal/health/summary.go`'s `Build` shape exactly): classifies severity via the existing `models.CapabilityBit` vocabulary (gaining `CAP_NET_ADMIN`/`CAP_NET_RAW` → warning, losing → info, any other bit change → an unnamed but still-surfaced `capdrift-other` finding), and — the one genuinely new signal, not just wiring — emits a `capdrift-coverage-gap` finding when an agent restarted recently. `watchCapChanges`' diff state (`prevCaps`) is in-memory and resets on every agent restart, so a capability change before the restart, or while the agent was down, produces no event; a new `AgentReport.AgentStartedAt` field (set once at agent boot) makes that blind spot detectable and this makes it explicit rather than silently under-reported.
  - Wired into `internal/alert/poller.go` as a fourth `collect(...)` source alongside `health`/`pathdiag`/`dropdiag`. New `GET /api/v1/ebpf/capdrift`, `netractl ebpf capdrift`, `netra_ebpf_capdrift` MCP read tool, and a new CAPABILITY DRIFT card on the Health page — no dedicated Process/Security page exists in this project, matching the established pattern of embedding diagnostics as cards on general pages rather than inventing a new page per signal.
  - Extended `docs/process-metadata.md` (this is a completion of that existing feature, not a new surface) rather than writing a separate doc.
  - Tests: `internal/capdrift/summary_test.go` — gained-capability is warning, lost is info, an unrelated bit change still fires (unnamed), coverage-gap fires only within the restart window, stale agents are skipped, topN truncates.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78)/build. Pure userland change — no kernel/BPF verification gate applies to this plan at all, unlike the just-completed eBPF plan.
- Version bumped to 0.27.35 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.34 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 3 item 11: capability-gated socket deny (CAP_NET_RAW/CAP_NET_ADMIN) — the final item of the entire "tons of eBPF feats" plan.**
  - Denies new `connect()`/`sendmsg()` attempts for any process with a configured capability currently effective. **Agent-sourced by design, not a kernel credential read** — reading `task_struct->real_cred->cap_effective` from `cgroup/connect4/6` would need an unstable CO-RE struct-offset read into unexported kernel internals (confirmed nothing like it exists anywhere in `bpf/netra_tc.c` before this). Instead the agent periodically scans `/proc` (gated by the existing `NETRA_PROCMETA_ENABLED` flag) and writes only the PIDs whose effective capabilities currently intersect the configured deny set into a new `capgate_pids` map, checked in `socket4`/`socket6` alongside `blocked_uids`/`blocked_comms` — same presence-flag shape, same allow-exception precedence. **Explicitly TOCTOU-caveated**: a capability change between scans isn't caught until the next sync — documented in `docs/capability-gated-deny.md`, same honesty standard as the QUIC-observed and ICMP-MTU caveats.
  - **A simpler design than first sketched, found while implementing.** The initial plan called for `capgate_pids` to store the raw `CapEff` bitmask with a second config map holding the deny-mask, ANDed together in BPF. Simplified to a plain presence flag — the agent does the bit-test in userspace during the scan (reusing `internal/procmeta`'s existing `Caps.HasAny()`) and only writes PIDs that already match, making the BPF-side check a single O(1) lookup with no second map, no in-kernel bitmask math. This is what "check it alongside `blocked_uids`/`blocked_comms`" actually meant once traced through.
  - New portable capability-name table, `models.CapabilityBit{CAP_NET_ADMIN: 12, CAP_NET_RAW: 13}` — deliberately **not** `internal/procmeta`'s existing (and much larger) `capNames` table, which is Linux-only (`//go:build linux`) and therefore unusable from `internal/store`/`internal/api`, which must build and test on every platform this project's tests run on (confirmed by trying it and hitting the build tag).
  - New `internal/procmeta.ListPIDs()` (a `/proc` directory scan — the one piece of `/proc` enumeration procmeta didn't already have; `Read(pid)` only ever read one already-known PID before this) and `internal/agent/capgate_linux.go`/`capgate_other.go`, mirroring `procmeta_linux.go`/`procmeta_other.go`'s existing platform-split pattern exactly.
  - Full stack: `internal/store/deny_capability.go` (mirrors `allow_identity.go`, participates in the generic ruleIndex/`ListRules`/`PatchRule`/`DeleteRule` system like `process`/`allow-process` do); `POST`/`POST .../delete /api/v1/ebpf/capability`, validated against the fixed 2-name vocabulary (not an arbitrary string, unlike process names); `netractl ebpf capability add|del CAP_NET_RAW|CAP_NET_ADMIN`; `netra_ebpf_capability_add`/`_delete` MCP tools; `web/src/pages/EBPF.tsx`'s new CAPABILITY card (a `<select>` of the 2 valid names, not free text).
  - Tests: `internal/store`'s `TestDeniedCapabilityCRUD`; `internal/api`'s `TestEBPFCapabilityRoundTrip` (including lowercase-input normalization)/`TestEBPFCapabilityRejectsUnknownName`; `internal/agent`'s `capgate_linux_test.go` (map-missing path, Linux-only — cannot be run on this development machine, only by CI) and `capgate_other_test.go` (the non-Linux stub's no-op contract, which *can* be run and was run here); `cmd/netractl`'s add/delete request-shape tests.
  - Doc-count sweep: 2 new MCP mutating tools (95→97 total stdio tools, 48→50 mutating); the unified rules table's flat-type count (17→18); capabilities endpoint and README enforcement-bullet updates.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78)/build. **Not verified against a real kernel verifier** — same standing caveat as every kernel change since 0.27.32, unresolved `zynera` deploy-path collision. This item's own risk profile is lower than item 10's (checked in `cgroup/connect4/6`, not the hot TC path; no new branch in an already-complex function — `capgate_pids` is one more lookup in a block that already does two), but it is still unverified against a real kernel and should not be treated as production-ready enforcement until it is.
  - **This completes the entire "tons of eBPF feats" plan**: Tier 1 (4/4 shipped, 0.27.28), Tier 2 (3/4 shipped + 1 dropped as infeasible, 0.27.29-31), Tier 3 (3/3 shipped, 0.27.32-34). Across all three tiers, nothing has been verified against a live kernel verifier since the `zynera` deploy-path collision blocked the primary agent-enabled host (`212.8.248.187`) partway through this work. That remains the single most important outstanding item before any of Tier 2's or Tier 3's kernel-side changes should be trusted in production.
- Version bumped to 0.27.34 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.33 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 3 item 10 of "tons of eBPF feats": SYN-drop mode ("block new connections, allow established"), scoped to exact-IP deny only.**
  - A new per-rule flag on **exact-IP** deny entries: once flagged, that address+direction only drops a genuinely new TCP connection attempt (SYN set, ACK clear) — any other TCP packet (including traffic on a connection Netra's own conntrack never saw the handshake for) is allowed through instead of unconditionally dropped. New `syndrop_v4`/`syndrop_v6` side-maps (keyed by address+direction, plain presence marker) — `blocked_v4`/`blocked_v6`/`blocked_ingress_v4`/`blocked_ingress_v6`'s pinned value type is untouched.
  - **Scope correction from the plan, made explicit rather than silently narrowed**: the plan described this as "a new field on exact-IP/CIDR deny rules." Implemented for exact-IP only — CIDR-matched deny isn't covered, since an LPM trie lookup returns a boolean-shaped match, not "which specific entry matched," so flagging one CIDR entry among many would need a second parallel LPM trie (or a different design entirely). Documented as a deliberate deferral in `docs/syn-drop.md` and the plan file, not a silent gap.
  - **A second correction found while implementing**: the plan's own design sketch said to extend `struct ct_state` with a handshake-established marker. Didn't need to — the override condition (`blocked && reason==REASON_EXACT && proto==IPPROTO_TCP && !syn_new && <side-map hit>`) reuses `syn_new`, a value every one of the four call sites already computes for conntrack purposes. `struct ct_state` stays exactly `{last_seen_ns, packets}`, unchanged.
  - **This is the highest-risk item in the whole plan** (the plan's own words) because, unlike item 9's function-signature change, it adds a genuine new conditional branch directly inside `handle_v4`/`handle_v6`'s hot TC/cgroup_skb path — the same program `docs/l7-metadata.md` documents a real prior verifier rejection near (a different location within it: the SNI/HTTP scan loops in a separate program, not this exact branch, but the same "this program is already complex" caution applies). Mitigated by: the new logic runs only inside the already-rare `blocked==1` branch, gated behind three cheap comparisons before the one extra hash lookup; a v6-specific `l4_ok` guard added after re-deriving the design (an unparseable TCP header leaves `syn_new` false the same way an established connection would, which could have let a malformed-header packet slip through a SYN-drop-flagged rule without the guard).
  - Full stack: `EBPFSynDropEntry{Address, Direction}` (direction is `egress`/`ingress` only — never `both`, since the kernel maps are inherently per-direction, unlike `EBPFCIDRRule`); `internal/store`'s `AddSynDrop`/`DelSynDrop` (equality-based, no generated ID, mirroring `AddCIDR`/`DelCIDR`'s shape but — like `AddNetPolRule`/`AddConnRateLimit` — deliberately outside the generic ruleIndex/`ListRules`/`PatchRule` system, since this is a low-cardinality flag list, not a primary rule type); `POST`/`POST .../delete /api/v1/ebpf/syn-drop`; `netractl ebpf syn-drop add|del IP egress|ingress`; `netra_ebpf_syn_drop_add`/`_delete` MCP tools; `web/src/pages/EBPF.tsx`'s new SYN-DROP MODE card.
  - Tests: `internal/store`'s `TestSynDropCRUD` (no-duplicate-on-re-add, same-address-different-direction is distinct); `internal/api`'s `TestEBPFSynDropRoundTrip`/`TestEBPFSynDropRejectsBoth`; `internal/agent`'s map-missing-path test; `cmd/netractl`'s add/delete request-shape tests.
  - Doc-count sweep: 2 new MCP mutating tools (93→95 total stdio tools, 46→48 mutating) fixed across `README.md`/`docs/mcp-integration.md`/`website/FeatureHighlights`, plus the reference table row and `/api/v1/ebpf/capabilities` enforcement list.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78)/build. **Not verified against a real kernel verifier** — no Linux/glibc toolchain available locally to compile the full BPF object, and the primary agent-enabled host (`212.8.248.187`) remains blocked by the unresolved `zynera` deploy-path collision. This is the item where that gap matters most so far: a genuinely new branch in the hottest path in the codebase, in the one program with documented prior verifier friction. Proceeding through Tier 3 per explicit instruction, but this specific item should be the first thing re-verified once a live kernel deploy is available again.
- Version bumped to 0.27.33 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.32 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 3 item 9 (first Tier-3 item) of "tons of eBPF feats": byte-rate (BPS) cap per destination.**
  - `EBPFRateLimit` (the existing "rate" flat rule — no new type) gains an independent `BPS uint32` field alongside `PPS`: a rule may set either, both, or neither is refused (all-zero is a no-op, same delete semantics as `PPS==0` used to be alone). New parallel BPF maps `rate_bps_v4`/`rate_bps_v6` (config) + `rate_byte_state_v4`/`rate_byte_state_v6` (per-second byte counters, reusing the existing `rate_state` struct — its `count` field holds cumulative bytes here) sit alongside the PPS maps, never resizing them.
  - **The mechanical part this plan item called out as the main risk**: changed `decide4`/`decide6`'s signature to take a packet-length argument, verified against the actual 8 call sites by direct grep before touching anything (`handle_v4`×2, `handle_v6`×2, `socket4`, `socket6`, XDP-ingress×2 — exactly matching the plan's own count). Only the two `handle_v4`/`handle_v6` TC/cgroup_skb call sites pass a real length; `socket4`/`socket6` and the XDP callers already pass `apply_rate=0` (rate limiting has always been packet-path-only), so the new argument is inert there — passed `0` or an already-computed length for consistency, not because it does anything.
  - Full stack: `internal/store`'s `SetRateLimit`/`ListRules`/`PatchRule` widened (not replaced) for BPS, sharing one new `validateRateLimit` helper between the PUT and PATCH handlers instead of duplicating the "at least one of pps/bps" rule; `internal/agent`'s `replaceByteRates`/`readByteRateDrops` mirror the existing PPS `replaceRates`/`readRateDrops` exactly; `netractl ebpf rate set IP PPS [BPS]` (PPS may be `0` when BPS is given, preserving old callers unchanged); `netra_ebpf_rate_set` MCP tool gained `bps`; `web/src/pages/EBPF.tsx`'s RATE CONTROL card and inline rule-edit form both gained a BPS input; Health page's new BYTE-RATE DROPS card, kept separate from CONNECTION-RATE DROPS and RATE DROPS since a destination's PPS/BPS caps fire independently and merging the counts would lose which one actually triggered. New `REASON_BPS` (13) reason code.
  - Tests: `internal/store`'s `TestSetRateLimitPPSAndBPSIndependent` (BPS-only survives, all-zero deletes); `internal/api`'s `TestEBPFRateAcceptsBPSOnly`/`TestEBPFRateRejectsAllZero`; `internal/agent`'s map-missing-path tests for both new functions; `cmd/netractl`'s `TestRateSetWithBPSPostsBothFields`/`BPSOnlyWithZeroPPS`/`RejectsZeroPPSWithoutBPS`.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, the C `abi_layout_test.c` guard (unaffected — no struct layout changed, only a function signature), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78)/build. **This is the first Tier 3 item — the plan's own verification requirement is a real kernel-verifier deploy before/after, given the touched hot path's documented prior verifier-rejection history (`docs/l7-metadata.md`).** That deploy remains blocked by the unresolved `zynera` deploy-path collision; proceeding into Tier 3 without it per explicit instruction, but the caveat stands until it's actually run.

## 0.27.31 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 2 item 8 (final item) of "tons of eBPF feats": cgroup new-connection-rate cap. Tier 2 now complete.**
  - New per-cgroup new-TCP-connection-rate ceiling: `conn_rate_limits`/`conn_rate_state` maps (`bpf/netra_tc.c`), checked in `socket4`/`socket6` right after the existing UID/comm block, gated to `proto==IPPROTO_TCP` — `cgroup/sendmsg4|6` (UDP) never reaches it, since a UDP `sendmsg()` isn't a new connection. Reuses the existing `rate_state` token-bucket struct verbatim; new `REASON_CONN_RATE` (12) reason code.
  - **Design correction found by checking the real analog before implementing**: the plan said to mirror "the allow-uid pattern," but `EBPFConnRateLimit`'s selector is compound (namespace/pod/kind/name/labels), not a scalar like a UID — the same shape `NetPolRule` already uses. Checked whether `NetPolRule` participates in the generic `ListRules`/`DeleteRule` rule browser (it doesn't — it has its own dedicated `AddNetPolRule`/`DelNetPolRule` with a generated ID) and mirrored that instead, rather than forcing a compound selector into a scalar-keyed CRUD pattern that doesn't fit it.

Agent side: `applyConnRateLimits` resolves every rule's selector against the node's live cgroup→workload table (the same `workload.Match` mechanism NetPol v2 already uses — same graceful degradation without a Kubernetes client) every sync, taking the **strictest** (lowest) `PerSecond` when multiple rules match one cgroup — "caps, not quotas that sum." `readConnRateDrops` surfaces fired caps as `AgentReport.ConnRateDrops`, named by workload identity when resolvable; Health page's new CONNECTION-RATE DROPS card.
  - **Small drive-by fix**: `internal/agent/agent.go`'s `reasonName()` was missing cases 10/11 (`REASON_NETPOL_RULE`/`REASON_NETPOL_DEFAULT_DENY`) even though those BPF constants have existed since NetPol v2 shipped — noticed while adding case 12, fixed alongside it.

Fixed `README.md`/`docs/mcp-integration.md`/`website/FeatureHighlights` (91→93 stdio tools, 44→46 mutating), added the two new tools' rows to `docs/mcp-integration.md`'s reference table, and added `udp_flow_health`/`quic_observed`/conn-rate-limit entries to `internal/api/server.go`'s `/api/v1/ebpf/capabilities` observability/enforcement lists (0.27.29/0.27.30 had shipped without updating that endpoint). New `docs/firewall.md` "Connection-rate limit" section (deliberately **not** folded into `docs/native-netpol.md` — this is a separate enforcement primitive that happens to reuse NetPol v2's selector shape, not a NetPol feature) plus a README enforcement bullet.
  - Tests: `internal/store`'s `TestConnRateLimitCRUD`; `internal/api`'s `TestRegisteredAPIRoutes` gained the two new routes; `internal/agent`'s `TestEffectiveConnRateLimitsMatchesSelector`/`PicksStrictest`/`IgnoresZeroPerSecond` (the selector-matching/strictest-wins logic pulled into a pure, map-I/O-free function specifically so it's unit-testable) plus map-missing-path tests; `cmd/netractl`'s `TestConnRateLimitAddPostsSelectorAndPerSecond`/`RejectsZeroPerSecond`/`DeleteHitsIDPath`.

## 0.27.30 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 2 item 6 of "tons of eBPF feats" dropped as infeasible; item 7 (QUIC-observed counter) shipped in its place.**
  - **TCP zero-window/window-scaling telemetry — verified infeasible before writing any code, not implemented.** Fetched mainline `include/uapi/linux/bpf.h` directly and confirmed `struct bpf_sock_ops` (what the existing `netra_sockops` program's `skops` is) has no `snd_wnd`/`snd_wscale`/`rcv_wscale` fields — the plan's stated mechanism doesn't exist. Checked the fallback too: `bpf_getsockopt(skops, SOL_TCP, TCP_INFO, ...)` would expose `tcpi_snd_wnd`/`tcpi_snd_wscale`/`tcpi_rcv_wscale` from `struct tcp_info`, but `net/core/filter.c`'s `bpf_sol_tcp_getsockopt()` optname whitelist for `sock_ops` getsockopt doesn't include `TCP_INFO` at all. The only remaining path — a CO-RE read into kernel-internal (non-UAPI) `struct tcp_sock` fields via a new tracing/kprobe hook — is exactly the "unstable struct-offset read, new hook type, real kernel-version fragility" pattern item 11 already rejected for a different feature. Presented this to the user with the verification trail; they chose to drop the item entirely rather than attempt the fragile route. Everything genuinely available in `bpf_sock_ops` (retrans/lost/sacked, RTT, cwnd) is already captured by the existing `tcp_health`/`tcp_pressure` maps, so there's no lesser signal to salvage either.
  - **QUIC-observed counter.** New `quic_observed` LRU map (`bpf/netra_tc.c`), counting UDP/443 packets whose first payload byte matches RFC 9000's long-header wire form (`0x80` top bit, `0x40` fixed bit), keyed by cgroup+remote-IP+port. **Not SNI extraction** — RFC 9001 mandatorily applies header protection to QUIC Initial packets, requiring HKDF-SHA256 + AES-128/ChaCha20, none of which exist as BPF helpers; `docs/quic-observed.md` states this explicitly, same honesty standard as `docs/udp-flow-health.md`'s dropped `send_errors` field. Populated from the same `cgroup_skb`-path call site as `udp_flow_health` (0.27.29) — real cgroup attribution, no new hook, no `decide4`/`decide6` change.
  - **Found and fixed a genuine key-layout bug of my own before it shipped**, caught by writing the literal-byte ABI test rather than trusting hand-derived offsets: `quic_observed_key`'s 3-byte pad after the 1-byte `family` field shifts `remote_addr` to byte offset 12, not 9 as first written — `TestDecodeQUICObservedABI`/`TestDecodeQUICObservedABIv6` now lock in the corrected offsets, and `bpf/tests/abi_layout_test.c` guards the struct sizes.
  - Full stack: `models.QUICObservedStat` + `AgentReport.QUICObserved`; `internal/agent/agent.go`'s `decodeQUICObserved`/`readQUICObserved`/`enrichQUICObserved` plus a `quic_observed` `mapNames` entry; `internal/health/summary.go` folds it into `NetworkHealthResponse.QUIC` and two summary counters (`quicObservedFlows`/`quicLongHeaderPackets`); `web/src/pages/Health.tsx` gained a QUIC OBSERVED tile and a QUIC-OBSERVED FLOWS table, inline in the same style as the TCP/DNS/UDP HEALTH sections.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, the C `abi_layout_test.c` guard, `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78)/build. Same deploy-verification caveat as 0.27.29 — no Linux/glibc headers available locally to compile the full BPF object, and the primary agent-enabled host (`212.8.248.187`) remains blocked by the unresolved `zynera` deploy-path collision.

## 0.27.29 — 2026-09-14

- **Tier 2 item 1 of "tons of eBPF feats": UDP flow health beyond DNS.** New `udp_flow_health` LRU map (`bpf/netra_tc.c`), tracking cgroup-attributed packets/bytes/last-seen per local/remote UDP endpoint, mirroring `tcp_health`'s key shape.
  - **Design correction found before writing any code.** The approved plan said to populate the map from "the existing `handle_v4`/`handle_v6` TC `IPPROTO_UDP` branch" — but direct code reading showed `handle_v4`/`handle_v6`'s `HOOK_TC` branch (the `tc/egress`/`tc/ingress` programs) hardcodes `cgroup_id = 0`, since TC classifiers have no cgroup context. Populating from there would have shipped a "cgroup-attributed" feature with zero real attribution. The fix: use the *other* existing invocation of the same two functions instead — `handle_l3()`, called from `cgroup_skb/egress`/`cgroup_skb/ingress` (`netra_cgroup_egress`/`netra_cgroup_ingress`) with `hook == HOOK_CGROUP` — which already computes a real `cgroup_id` via `bpf_get_current_cgroup_id()` for the exact same `update_flow()` call site that already populates `workload_flow_stats`. The new `update_udp_flow_health()` call sits right next to that existing call, guarded to `IPPROTO_UDP` and unblocked packets, adding one LRU lookup+update to an already-attached hook — no new hook type, no `decide4`/`decide6` signature change, no hot-path (`tc/*`) involvement at all.
  - **`send_errors` dropped from the original plan's value struct** — no real signal exists for it. `cgroup/sendmsg4/6` runs before the packet is queued (can't see a later delivery failure), and the existing per-interface `icmp_errors` map isn't keyed per-flow. Documented explicitly in the new `docs/udp-flow-health.md` rather than shipping a counter that would always read zero. Same honesty standard as the plan's QUIC-observed reframe and the existing ICMP MTU peer-claim caveat.
  - Full stack: `models.UDPFlowHealthStat` + `AgentReport.UDPFlowHealth`; `internal/agent/agent.go`'s `decodeUDPFlowHealth`/`readUDPFlowHealth`/`enrichUDPFlowHealth` (cgroup→workload identity join, same pattern as every other health reader) plus a `udp_flow_health` `mapNames` entry; `internal/health/summary.go` folds it into `NetworkHealthResponse.UDP` and three new summary counters (`udpFlows`/`udpPackets`/`udpBytes`); `web/src/pages/Health.tsx` gained a UDP PULSE metrics tile and a UDP FLOW HEALTH table, inline in the same style as the existing TCP/DNS HEALTH sections (not a separate diagnostics component — there's no extra client-side classification logic to justify one, unlike DNS's RCODE handling).
  - Tests: `bpf/tests/abi_layout_test.c` gained `udp_flow_key`/`udp_flow_value` size guards (56/24 bytes); `internal/agent`'s `TestDecodeUDPFlowHealthABI` (literal-byte offset check), `TestUDPFlowHealthUnavailableWhenMapMissing`, `TestEnrichUDPFlowHealthSkipsZeroCgroup`.

The full BPF object was not compiled locally this pass — no Linux/glibc headers available on this machine, matching how `bpf/netra_tc.c` has always been verified (CI's Ubuntu runner, or `Dockerfile.agent`'s build on a live Linux deploy host) rather than on macOS. **Still pending a real kernel-verifier deploy** — same caveat as every kernel change this session; the primary agent-enabled host (`212.8.248.187`) remains blocked by the unresolved `zynera` deploy-path collision from earlier in this session.

## 0.27.28 — 2026-09-13

- **Tier 1 of "tons of eBPF feats"**: the first of a three-tier feature plan (11 new features spanning enforcement, observability, and security, researched and designed via parallel Explore/Plan passes against this project's own stated boundaries — not a CNI/QoS/L7-proxy/IDS-IPS, observe-first, no payload inspection, no active probing). Tier 1 is the pure-Go/additive-only slice; Tiers 2-3 follow separately, with Tier 3 gated on a real kernel-verifier deploy given it touches the hot `decide4`/`decide6` TC path.
  - **Cross-signal correlation.** New `internal/health/correlate.go`: when two or more independent anomaly *kinds* (TCP, DNS, ICMP, bpf-maps-missing, etc.) hit the same workload or node in one build cycle, a new `correlated-degradation` finding is appended (never replacing the individual findings) naming them via a new `RelatedKinds []string` field on `NetworkHealthAnomaly`. The grouping key deliberately strips the "→ remote:port" suffix `subject()` bakes into TCP/DNS anomaly subjects (`workloadKey()`), since otherwise a DNS-failure anomaly on `ns/pod → db.internal:53` would never correlate with a TCP-retransmit anomaly on the same workload's `ns/pod → 10.0.0.8:443` — the exact "DNS failures + TCP retransmits on the same workload" case this feature exists for. Renders automatically through the existing generic HEALTH SIGNALS card and AI brief pipeline — no new UI or explain.go wiring needed, since `internal/health.Build()`'s anomalies were already never part of the `netractl explain` pipeline (a distinct system from `internal/ai`'s AI-narrated single-finding "Explain" button), a scoping correction from the original design sketch.
  - **One-shot workload quarantine.** `netractl ebpf netpol quarantine [selector flags] [--allow-peer IP[:PORT[/PROTO]]]... [--lease DURATION] [--confirm-risk RISK] [--allow-no-rules]` — a **client-side-only** convenience composing three already-fully-safety-gated endpoints (netpol v2 enable, netpol rule add per `--allow-peer`, then the mandatory plan → confirm-risk → apply default-deny sequence) into one command. Deliberately **introduces no new server-side endpoint and bypasses none of the existing safety machinery** — discovered mid-design that `SetNetPolDefaultDeny` is documented as "the single highest-blast-radius mutation in the firewall feature" with a mandatory two-step preflight (receipt token, critical-risk refusal on zero covering allow rules, `X-Netra-Confirm-Risk` header), so quarantine's whole design is to preserve that, not shortcut it — mirroring `policy apply --confirm-risk`'s existing composition pattern exactly. `--allow-peer` requires an exact IPv4 (`NetPolRule` has no DNS-name peer support), so quarantining a workload that still needs DNS requires naming the cluster DNS service IP explicitly (`--allow-peer 10.96.0.10:53/UDP`) — not guessed. Web UI and MCP tool deliberately deferred: the web NetPol v2 card already has an equivalent manual multi-step flow, and this MCP framework's tools are 1:1 single-endpoint proxies with no multi-step composition support.
  - **Bulk deny-list import.** `POST /api/v1/ebpf/deny/import` (`netractl ebpf deny import FILE`, new `internal/api/deny_import.go`) bulk-populates exact-IP/CIDR/DNS/SNI deny primitives from an operator-supplied list (line format `TYPE VALUE [DIRECTION]`, `#` comments, up to 1000 entries), dispatching every entry through the exact same per-type validators (`netip.ParseAddr`, `netip.ParsePrefix`, `normalizeDNSName`) the single-rule endpoints already use — zero new validation or detection logic. Reports per-entry success/failure rather than all-or-nothing, since a real operator-supplied list usually has a few bad lines.
  - **Shield connection-attempt-rate anomaly.** New `attempts uint64` field on the existing `shield_source_hit_value` BPF struct (`bpf/netra_tc.c`, 16→24 bytes, ABI-guarded in `bpf/tests/abi_layout_test.c`), incremented unconditionally in `shield_rate_ok()` for every SYN packet from a source — regardless of pass/drop verdict, unlike the existing `denied` field, which only increments once the token bucket is empty. This means the new `shield-connection-rate` anomaly (`internal/shielddiag/summary.go`) can fire in `audit` mode or when `syn_pps` is disabled, where `denied` (and `shield-source-flood`) never fires at all. Found and fixed a related bug while implementing this: the per-source top-N list was sorted by `denied` both agent-side (`internal/agent/agent.go`) and controller-side, which would silently truncate away a high-`attempts`/zero-`denied` source before this new anomaly could ever see it — since `attempts >= denied` always holds, sorting by `attempts` instead is strictly more inclusive and was changed in both places.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (including all new/extended Go tests above), the C `abi_layout_test.c` guard plus the 3 existing host tests (unaffected), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78)/build. The Shield `attempts` field is a real, additive BPF ABI change (map value struct grew) — still pending a live kernel-verifier deploy, same caveat as every kernel change this session.
- Version bumped to 0.27.28 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.27 — 2026-09-13

- **Closed four follow-up gaps surfaced by a fresh "what else is missing" audit** (three parallel research passes plus one design pass) after the rapid allow-exception/diagnostics wave earlier today. Unlike the earlier stale-kit merges, none of this depended on external patches — all four items were found and closed directly.
  - **Doc/tool-count drift** (0.27.23-0.27.26 added 6 MCP tools and 2 flat rule types since the last count fix): `README.md` and `website/FeatureHighlights` corrected to 91 stdio tools / 44 mutating (47 read unchanged); `docs/mcp-integration.md`'s two "40 mutating tool" mentions fixed to 44, and its per-tool reference table gained the 4 rows it was missing entirely (`netra_ebpf_allow_uid_add`/`_delete`, `netra_ebpf_allow_process_add`/`_delete` — these tools existed in `cmd/netra-mcp/tools_mutate.go` but had zero documentation); `docs/firewall.md`'s "15 flat types" corrected to 17, with UID/process allow-exceptions added to the type list.
  - **`internal/ai/draft.go` (the "Ask Netra" natural-language rule parser) learned the newest rule types.** Previously "allow port 443", "except UID 1000", and "allow process coredns" all fell through to `Understood: false` even though every other newly-added mutating endpoint already had full CLI/API/web coverage. Added allow-checks to the port/UID/process branches (mirroring the exact pattern the IPv4/IPv6-allow branches already used), plus a bonus CIDR-allow branch (CIDR was deny-only despite `/api/v1/ebpf/allow-cidr` existing since earlier this session) and a drive-by fix: the process-deny branch was sending `Body: {"comm": ...}` but the real endpoint decodes JSON key `"name"` — a latent, previously-harmless bug (nothing auto-submits `Body` today) now fixed on both the deny and new allow branches. Added `TestDraftRuleAllowCIDR`/`AllowPort`/`AllowUID`/`AllowProcess` plus regression guards confirming the matching deny phrasing is unaffected.
  - **`bpf-maps-missing` and `rate-drop` now flow through the explain/investigation system**, matching the coverage ICMP/DNS diagnostics already got. New `cmd/netractl/bpfhealth.go` (`bpfMapsMissingFinding`, `rateDropFinding`, `includesRateDrops()`) mirrors `icmp.go`/`dns.go` exactly; wired into `buildExplain` and the `Limitations` list. `rate-drop` uniquely also matches `--destination` scope (unlike ICMP/`bpf-maps-missing`, which are node-wide-only) since a rate-drop row already names a destination IP. Byte-identical mirror on the web side: new `web/src/lib/bpfhealth.ts`, wired into `web/src/lib/explain.ts`. `web/src/pages/Health.tsx`'s "BPF OBJECT" and "RATE DROPS" cards gained the `<ExplainFinding>` button the "ICMP PULSE" card already had. `docs/explain.md` gained the two new finding kinds plus a scope-boundary sentence; `docs/network-health.md` — which had zero mentions of either anomaly despite them existing since 0.27.22/0.27.23 — gained two threshold bullets. New `cmd/netractl/bpfhealth_test.go` and `web/src/lib/bpfhealth.test.ts`, plus matching additions to `explain.test.ts`.
  - **NetPol v2 UID/process allow-exception parity — investigated and recorded as deliberately deferred, not implemented.** Verified directly in `bpf/netra_tc.c` that this is a genuine architectural mismatch, not just unfinished work: `netpol_rules4` is looked up inside `cgroup_skb/egress|ingress` hooks on an already-built `__sk_buff`, where ingress traffic has no reliable process-identity context, while UID/comm matching is enforced in the structurally different `cgroup/connect4|connect6|sendmsg4|sendmsg6` (`bpf_sock_addr`) hooks — the only place `bpf_get_current_uid_gid()`/`bpf_get_current_comm()` are valid (exactly why the project already caches identity into `socket_owner` at connect time). Documented in `docs/native-netpol.md` alongside the existing CIDR/IPv6 follow-up note; no code change. Port-only NetPol v2 allow-exceptions don't have this mismatch and are noted as a smaller, separate future candidate.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (including all new/extended Go tests above), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (78/78, up from 70)/build. No BPF/kernel changes in this release — nothing to redeploy for kernel-verifier purposes.

## 0.27.25 — 2026-09-13

- **Applied `Netra-eBPF-Plus-v2.patch`** — a genuine `git diff`-format patch (unlike this session's four prior "kit" directory snapshots), and of a different, higher character: no `apply_rate`/Shield-IPv6/dual-stack-rate/`explain`-deletion regressions anywhere in it, complete matching test coverage for every new code path, careful security handling, and two internal validation notes (`docs/internal/validation-icmp-docker.md`, `docs/internal/validation-dns-plus.md`) candidly disclosing what wasn't tested (no live kernel-verifier load, no real Docker daemon, no published GitHub PR). Three new features:
  - **ICMP error diagnostics.** New `bpf/netra_icmp.h` fixed-header parser (quoted original packets are never read or retained) and a new additive `icmp_errors` LRU map (4096 entries, node/interface-scoped, never workload-attributed) populated at the existing TC ingress/egress hooks for both IPv4 and IPv6. Surfaces MTU/packet-too-big, destination-unreachable, time-exceeded, and parameter-problem errors as new `netractl explain`/dashboard-Explain finding kinds (`icmp-pmtu`, `icmp-unreachable`, `icmp-time-exceeded`, `icmp-parameter-problem`), available only at node-wide/`--all` scope to avoid false workload attribution. New Health page "ICMP DIAGNOSTICS" card (distinct from the existing "ICMP PULSE" type-histogram card). See `docs/icmp-diagnostics.md`.
  - **DNS response diagnostics.** No new eBPF map or parser — the kernel has emitted `dns-response` events with a base-header RCODE and query-response latency since dns_pending tracking shipped (`internal/agent/agent.go`'s `eventName(5)`), but nothing ever classified them. Adds NOERROR/FORMERR/SERVFAIL/NXDOMAIN/NOTIMP/REFUSED/unknown-RCODE classification with a next-check per code, wired into `netractl explain` and a new Health page "DNS RESPONSE DIAGNOSTICS" card. Verified the underlying event/field ABI (`type`, `dnsRcode`, `latencyUs` JSON tags on `models.FastPathEvent`) already existed and matches before trusting this was live data, not a feature with no data source. See `docs/dns-response-diagnostics.md`.
  - **Local Docker-name discovery for `netractl explain --docker NAME --node NODE`.** CLI-only (requires the Docker host's Unix socket, PID namespace, and cgroup-v2 mount — not exposed via the web dashboard). Notably careful implementation: exact container name/full-64-char-ID only (no abbreviated IDs), the Netra bearer token is never sent to the Docker socket, inspect responses are bounded to 4 MiB and redirects are rejected, only a handful of identity fields are decoded (raw inspect JSON — which can contain env vars and registry credentials — is never printed), and the container identity (ID/PID/start-time/cgroup) is rechecked after fetching reports to detect a restart or cgroup migration mid-diagnosis rather than silently diagnosing a replaced container. See `docs/explain.md#local-docker-name-discovery`.
  - **Hand-merged 3 files** whose patch hunks no longer matched current `main` due to this session's own kit #2–#4 growth in the same regions (additive drift, not conflicting regressions):
    - `bpf/netra_tc.c`: inserted the new `icmp_errors` map/`record_icmp_error()`/parse call sites into `handle_v4`/`handle_v6` without touching `decide4`/`decide6`'s `apply_rate` parameter or any Shield/allow/rate logic added since.
    - `internal/agent/agent.go`: added `"icmp_errors"` to `mapNames` (kept `shield_protected6`, `allowed_ports`, etc.) and wired `readICMPErrors()` into `syncAndReport` alongside the existing `RateDrops`/`MissingMaps` fields from 0.27.23.
    - `web/src/pages/Health.tsx`: placed the new `<DNSDiagnostics>` card right after DNS Pulse, and `<ICMPDiagnostics>` right after the existing ICMP Pulse histogram card — both alongside, not replacing, 0.27.23's BPF-maps-missing/RATE DROPS cards.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (internal/agent now has its own test file for the first time), the new `bpf/tests/icmp_parse_test.c` plus the two pre-existing C host tests (`ipv6_walk_test`, `l7_parse_test`) and the ABI layout guard, `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (70/70, up from 56 — matches the patch's own claimed count exactly)/build. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` updated to compile and run the new ICMP parser test. Real kernel-verifier load of the new `icmp_errors` map still requires a live agent-enabled deploy — not run for this release, consistent with every kernel change this session.
- Also added the missing `icmp_errors` map description to `bpf/README.md` and an "ICMP error diagnostics" entry to `/api/v1/ebpf/capabilities`'s observability list — the incoming patch didn't touch either file, but this session's convention keeps both in sync with every new map.

Because nothing in the kernel program actually changed from the already kernel-verifier-validated 0.27.21 state, this release did not require another verifier-check deploy to the agent-enabled host.
  - **Genuinely new content, all in files untouched by kits 1/2**, was found and applied:
    - `internal/api/metrics.go`: five new `/metrics` Prometheus gauges — `netra_fastpath_allowed_ipv4`, `netra_fastpath_allowed_ipv6`, `netra_fastpath_allowed_cidrs`, `netra_fastpath_blocked_ingress_ipv4`, `netra_fastpath_blocked_ingress_ipv6` — surfacing the allow-exception and ingress-deny fast-path maps added in 0.27.15/0.27.21, which had never been exported as metrics despite every sibling deny/CIDR/port/rate map already being covered. Added `TestMetricsFastpathGauges`, asserting the exact gauge lines against a store seeded with one rule of each new kind.
    - `internal/health/summary.go`: new ICMP anomaly detection in `anomalies()` — cumulative `echo-request` count ≥ 10,000 emits a `icmp-echo` warning, cumulative `dest-unreach` count ≥ 1,000 emits an `icmp-unreach` warning, both keyed by node and summing v4+v6 counters (`AgentReport.ICMPTypes`/`ICMP6Types`, wired end-to-end in 0.27.21 but never actually alerted on). Added `TestBuildFindsICMPAnomalies` and `TestBuildIgnoresICMPBelowThreshold` to `internal/health/summary_test.go`, covering both the trigger and the threshold boundary.
    - `web/src/pages/Health.tsx`: new "ICMP PULSE" card rendering the per-node ICMP/ICMPv6 type histogram (top 20 by count, graceful empty-state when no agent has reported any ICMP traffic yet), wired to the new anomalies via the existing `ExplainFinding` affordance.
    - `internal/api/server.go`: `ebpfRuleLimits` gained entries for the five rule kinds introduced in 0.27.21 (`allowIPv4`, `allowIPv6`, `allowCidr`, `exactIngressIPv4`, `exactIngressIPv6`) — previously unlimited by omission; and `ebpfCapabilities`'s `observability` list now mentions the ICMPv4/ICMPv6 type histograms.
  - **Fixed a real, kit-flagged display bug unrelated to any of the kit's own diff content**: the unified Firewall rules table's value-extraction `useMemo` in `web/src/pages/EBPF.tsx` only special-cased `r.type === 'cidr'` for reading `.cidr`, so `allow-cidr` rows (added 0.27.21) rendered a blank value column instead of the CIDR. Fixed to also match `r.type === 'allow-cidr'`.
  - **Rejected one more kit change as unsafe rather than applying it wholesale**: the kit's `EBPF.tsx` diff also tried to add the five new rule types (`ip4-in`, `ip6-in`, `allow4`, `allow6`, `allow-cidr`) to `EDIT_FIELDS`, enabling an in-place Edit UX for them — but `internal/store/store.go`'s `PatchRule` switch (`case "ip4", "ip6", "dns", "sni", "process":`) was never extended to support these types by this kit or any prior one. Applying the frontend half alone would have shipped an Edit button that always 400s. Left `EDIT_FIELDS` unchanged; these rule kinds remain add/delete-only, consistent with how allow-exceptions have worked since 0.27.15.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (including the two new test files above), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build.
- Version bumped to 0.27.22 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.21 — 2026-09-13

- **Merged a second external eBPF PR kit** (`netra-ebpf-pr 2`) — its own description undersold it as a re-issue of the already-merged 0.27.15 feature set, but the actual diff carried real, additional value on top of that, plus several regressions of work done since. Diffed every file individually against current `main` rather than trusting either claim, per this session's established kit-merge discipline.
  - **ICMP histogram wired end-to-end for the first time.** `icmp_type_stats` (0.27.15) was populated by the kernel but never read by anything — confirmed dead since merge. This kit adds the missing half: a new `icmp6_type_stats` BPF map (parity with the existing v4 one), `bump_icmp_map`/`bump_icmp6_type` wired into the IPv6 packet walk, agent-side `readICMPTypeStats`/`icmpTypeName` (human labels like `echo-request`, `dest-unreach`), `AgentReport.ICMPTypes`/`ICMP6Types`, and — the actual payoff — `internal/api/ai.go` now merges both into `Snapshot.TopICMP` (v6 entries prefixed `v6:`, capped at 8), so Ask Netra and the on-call brief can finally narrate ICMP activity instead of silently ignoring a histogram the kernel had been building the whole time.
  - **Directional exact-IP deny.** `POST /api/v1/ebpf/deny` gains an optional `direction` field (`egress`\|`ingress`\|`both`, default `egress`), backed by new `blocked_ingress_v4`/`v6` BPF maps and `store.AddBlockedIngress`/`AddBlockedIngressIPv6`. Closes a real asymmetry — CIDR and port deny already supported direction, exact-IP deny didn't. Added a web UI direction selector on the existing exact-IP deny card.
  - **CIDR-shaped allow-exceptions.** New `POST /api/v1/ebpf/allow-cidr` (+ `/delete`), backed by `allowed_cidr_v4`/`v6` LPM-trie maps and `store.AddAllowedCIDR`/`DelAllowedCIDR`, checked in `decide4`/`decide6` right after the existing exact-IP allow check. Closes the mirror-image gap: deny already had exact+CIDR forms, allow only had exact. Added a matching web UI card and two new `netra-mcp` tools (`netra_ebpf_allow_cidr_add`/`_delete`, mutating tool count now 42).
  - **Rejected several regressions the kit's stale base reintroduced**, keeping this session's own fixes intact: `decide6()`'s `apply_rate` bug (fixed in 0.27.15, the kit's copy was missing the parameter again — same fix reapplied to the new call sites it needed); the entire Shield IPv6 feature (0.27.16 — `ProtectedIPv6`, `shield_protected6`, its agent replication, MCP field); dual-stack rate limiting (0.27.16 — kit reverted `ebpfRateSet`/`Delete` to IPv4-only); the AI draft-rule IPv6 fix (0.27.20 — kit's `draft.go`/`draft_test.go` predate it entirely, rejected both files outright); and, for the 9th+ time this session, `cmd/netractl/main.go`'s raw diff tried to delete the `explain` command.
  - **Found and fixed two more real, previously-undiscovered bugs while merging and testing this**, neither caused by the kit itself:
    - `dependencyGraph()` (called by `aiSnapshot`, `insightsDependencies`, `insightsRecommendations`, and others) called `s.kube.ListPods` with no nil-check, unlike every other `s.kube` consumer in the package — a standalone-eBPF deployment with no Kubernetes/Cilium access (`s.kube == nil`, a real supported mode per this project's own README) would panic the request instead of degrading gracefully. Fixed with a nil-check returning a plain error; `aiSnapshot` already treated a `dependencyGraph` error as "skip these fields," so the fix required no changes there.
    - `ebpfDenyDelete` only ever cleared the egress `blocked_v4`/`v6` maps — adding a web UI "remove" chip for the new ingress-deny entries would have silently done nothing. Fixed to clear whichever direction(s) actually contain the address, so a single `DELETE /api/v1/ebpf/deny/{ip}` works regardless of how it was added.
  - Also updated `docs/standalone-ebpf.md`, `docs/firewall.md` (rule-type count 11 → 14), `docs/mcp-integration.md`, `bpf/README.md`, and `README.md`'s feature list for all of the above.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (including new regression tests for the nil-kube panic, the ICMP snapshot merge, directional deny add/delete, and the allow-CIDR round-trip), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build. Local BPF compile remains impossible on this Mac (no kernel headers); the new maps and `decide4`/`decide6` changes still need a real kernel-verifier check on next agent-enabled deploy.
- Version bumped to 0.27.21 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.20 — 2026-09-13

- **Real kernel BPF verifier validation, finally.** Deployed 0.27.19 to the primary, agent-enabled host (`212.8.248.187`, `NETRA_AGENT_ENABLED=true`) — the only way to get an authentic verifier-load check for this session's kernel changes, since local compilation isn't possible on this Mac. Result: the `netra-agent` DaemonSet rolled out clean, 0 restarts, no crash-loop. All core datapath hooks (`cgroup-egress`, `cgroup-ingress`, `connect4`, `connect6`, `sockops`, `tcx-egress`, `tcx-ingress`, `udp-sendmsg4`, `udp-sendmsg6`, `xdp`) attached successfully — these are exactly the programs that reference the new `allowed_v4`/`allowed_v6`, `rate_v6`/`rate_state_v6`, and `icmp_type_stats` maps added in 0.27.15. The only verifier rejection in the logs was the pre-existing, already-documented L7 SNI/HTTP program limitation (`docs/l7-metadata.md`), unrelated to this session's work, and the agent degraded gracefully exactly as designed. Caveat: this deploy did not set `NETRA_XDP_SHIELD=true`, so `shield_protected6` (0.27.16) specifically wasn't exercised — Shield's XDP variant only attaches when explicitly enabled; that one still needs its own verification pass.
- **Found and fixed a real character-dropping bug in the shared investigation-filter inputs** (`web/src/components/InvestigationFilters.tsx`, used by the Connections and Workloads pages' namespace/pod/node/search filters) — found while testing the Workloads page's filters live. The input's `value` was bound directly to route state (`scope[key]`) that only updates via the browser's async `hashchange` event, fired after `navigate()` sets `window.location.hash`. Any re-render landing between a keystroke and that event resets the DOM value to the still-stale `scope[key]`, silently dropping whatever was just typed. Reproduced concretely: typing "kube-system" fast left only "m" (the last character) in the field and the URL; slowing to 1-second gaps between keystrokes worked fine, confirming the race. Notably, the existing Playwright smoke test (`web/tests/investigation-smoke.cjs`) never caught this because it uses `.fill()` — which sets the whole value atomically in one event — not real character-by-character typing, so the race window it depends on never existed in that test. Fixed by giving the input local echo state (`draft`) that updates synchronously on every keystroke, independent of the hash round-trip, while `navigate()` still pushes the change to the URL for downstream consumers as before.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build.
- Version bumped to 0.27.20 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.19 — 2026-09-13

- **Found and fixed a page-wide error-visibility bug in the Firewall dashboard**, via another round of live Chrome testing. `web/src/pages/EBPF.tsx` uses one shared `err` state for roughly a dozen different actions across the whole page (workload-scope preview/apply, rules patch/delete/rollback/history, default-deny plan/apply, and the background data poll itself) — but it was rendered in exactly one place (inside the unrelated ENFORCEMENT LEASE card), and the page's own 5-second background poll unconditionally cleared it (`setErr('')`) on every successful tick regardless of whether the user had even seen it. In practice this meant a validation error like "Choose at least one workload selector" (clicking Preview under WORKLOAD SCOPE with all fields empty) would render nowhere near the button the user clicked, and would usually be gone before they noticed — confirmed by testing with a tight click→read round-trip; a slightly slower check showed nothing at all. Found by deliberately testing an edge case (an empty-selector Preview click) that the earlier phases of testing hadn't covered.
  - Fixed by (1) moving the error banner to a page-top, full-width location so it's visible immediately regardless of which action produced it, and (2) giving `load()` a `silent` parameter used only by the 5s background-poll `setInterval` call, so a routine poll tick no longer clears an error the user hasn't acted on yet — while explicit loads (initial mount, and the refresh every mutating action already triggers via `call()`) still clear and re-surface errors normally.
  - Also exercised, this round, every remaining untested Firewall add-flow (CIDR, port, UID, process, DNS, SNI — all correct), Shield apply, NetPol v2 enable + rule add, the default-deny plan/preflight step (stopped short of actually activating default-deny on live workloads), and Insights baseline capture/clear — all working correctly, no further bugs found.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build.
- Version bumped to 0.27.19 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.18 — 2026-09-13

- **Found and fixed two real bugs via live Chrome testing** of the deployed dashboard, following on from the earlier phases' verification:
  - **AI draft-rule feature never handled IPv6 at all.** `internal/ai/draft.go` defined a `reIPv6` regex but never referenced it anywhere — `DraftRule("allow 2001:db8::55")` (and any IPv6 deny/rate/CIDR phrasing) silently fell through to "could not extract a safe exact match", even though the corresponding IPv4 phrasing worked and the backend has supported IPv6 deny/CIDR for a while, and now rate/allow since 0.27.15/16. Found by testing the exact IPv6 allow phrasing added in 0.27.15 against the live `/api/v1/ai/draft` endpoint. Fixed by adding a full IPv6 CIDR regex (`reCIDR6`) and an IPv6 address branch mirroring the existing IPv4 one (allow/rate/deny), and added `TestDraftRuleIPv6` covering all four kinds.
  - **The dashboard's Policies page crashed to a blank screen** when running Preflight against a policy that doesn't exist yet on the cluster (the exact default sample the page ships with, on a cluster with no prior CiliumNetworkPolicy of that name). Root cause: `internal/policy/plan.go`'s `AnalyzeChange`, in its "new policy" early-return branch, never set `RemovedDestinations`, leaving it as Go's nil-slice zero value — which marshals to JSON `null`, not `[]`. `web/src/pages/Policies.tsx` unconditionally read `.length`/`.map` on that field with no null guard, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'length')` and taking down the entire React render tree. This bug is invisible to Go unit tests that only check `len(x)==0` (true for both nil and empty slices) — only a JSON-marshal-level check catches it. Fixed by initializing all four `ChangePlan` slice fields (`Changes`/`Warnings`/`AddedDestinations`/`RemovedDestinations`) to non-nil at construction, added `TestAnalyzeChangeNewPolicyHasNoNullSlices` (asserts on the marshaled JSON, not just `len()`), and added defensive `|| []` fallbacks in `Policies.tsx` as defense-in-depth matching this codebase's established null-safe pattern elsewhere (`EBPF.tsx`'s `cfg?.blockedIPv4?.length||0`).
  - Both bugs were found by actually exercising the live dashboard end-to-end via Chrome (typing real questions into Ask Netra / Explain / Policies, not just visually screenshotting pages) rather than relying on unit tests or static review alone — reinforcing that this is a meaningfully different and complementary check.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build, `helm lint`.
- Version bumped to 0.27.18 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

  - **`docs/high-availability.md`**: "exact IPv4 emergency deny entries" in the durable-state list was narrow/stale (deny has been dual-stack for a while, predating this session, and the list didn't cover allow/rate/Shield/NetPol either) — folded into the adjacent "fast-path configuration" bullet with an explicit list of what that covers.

## 0.27.16 — 2026-09-13

- **Closed four gaps found in a "what's missing" audit** of the whole product surface (docs, backend API, web dashboard, CLI, MCP, and the eBPF fast-path controls) — three parallel research passes (TODO/gap-marker sweep, marketing-vs-implementation check, API/web/CLI/MCP surface-parity check) found the codebase unusually honest overall, but surfaced these concrete, closeable gaps:
  - **Finished IPv6 rate limiting end-to-end.** The 0.27.15 eBPF kit added full IPv6 destination-PPS support in the kernel (`rate_v6`/`rate_state_v6`, `decide6()`'s `apply_rate` gate) and the agent (`replaceRates()` dispatch by address family), but `internal/api/server.go`'s `ebpfRateSet`/`ebpfRateDelete`/`ebpfRulePatch`("rate") handlers still hard-rejected any non-IPv4 destination with `!a.Is4()` — the feature was unreachable through every surface even though the plumbing underneath was already done. Relaxed all three checks to accept any valid `netip.Addr`; added `TestEBPFRateAcceptsIPv6` (`internal/api/ebpf_rate_test.go`) to guard the regression; updated `netractl`'s usage text and the Firewall dashboard's Rate Control card copy/placeholder to say "IPv4 or IPv6" instead of IPv4-only.
  - **Added web UI for three backend features that had none.** `ebpf allow` (add/delete), `ebpf ipv6` (extension-header/fragmentation diagnostics), and `ebpf interfaces` (per-interface flow attribution) were all fully wired through the API, `netractl`, and `netra-mcp`, but a dashboard-only user had no way to see or use them — genuinely undiscoverable features. Added an Allow-exception card (mirrors the existing exact-IP deny card) and two new read-only diagnostics cards to `EBPF.tsx`, following the page's existing `datatable-scroll`/`datahead obs`/`datarow obs` pattern.
  - **Extended XDP Shield's protected-IP list to IPv6.** Shield's per-IP protection was IPv4-only (`ProtectedIPv4`, `shield_protected4`); IPv6 traffic could only be protected via the blanket, non-selective `protectAll`. Added a `shield_protected6` BPF map (mirrors `shield_protected4`'s key/value shape) and wired it into `netra_xdp_shield`'s IPv6 branch, which previously just returned `XDP_PASS` for any non-`protectAll` IPv6 packet with the comment "v6 protected set omitted in v1". Added `ShieldConfig.ProtectedIPv6`, agent-side replication into the new map, API validation, a `netractl ebpf shield --ip6` flag, updated the `netra_ebpf_shield_set` MCP tool description, and updated `docs/tcx-and-shield.md`. Also fixed a real, previously-latent bug found while wiring this up: `internal/store/store.go`'s `cloneConfig` only deep-copied `Shield.ProtectedIPv4`, not a hypothetical `ProtectedIPv6` field — the exact class of bug this project hit for real in 0.25.0 (forgetting to clone `Shield`/`NetPolDenies`). Fixed before it could ever surface, since the field didn't exist until this release.
  - **Fixed two stale documentation claims.** `README.md` said "85 stdio tools ... 47 read ... 38 mutating" (actual: 87/47/40, matching `docs/mcp-integration.md`'s already-correct figures) and "IPv6 extension-header walking is not implemented in v0.14" (it shipped in v0.16, per `docs/ipv6-extension-headers.md`). `website/src/components/FeatureHighlights/index.tsx` still said "~62 stdio tools". All corrected.
  - **Explicitly left out of scope**: NetPol v2 IPv6 peer rules, which the code itself already flags as a follow-up (`models.go`: "CIDR-shaped peers and IPv6 are explicit follow-ups") since it requires a whole new `netpol_v2_lookup6` rule-matching engine wired into `decide6()`, not one more map lookup — and this project has real precedent (`docs/l7-metadata.md`'s permanently-disabled L7 SNI/Host scan on some kernels) for BPF-verifier rejections from added program complexity.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...`, `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build. Real kernel-verifier validation of the new `shield_protected6` map still requires a live deploy with `NETRA_AGENT_ENABLED=true`, not done as part of this release per the user's preference not to deploy to the agent-enabled host right now.
- Version bumped to 0.27.16 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.15 — 2026-09-13

- **Merged an external eBPF PR kit** (`netra-ebpf-pr`) — the first kit this session to touch the kernel program itself (`bpf/netra_tc.c`), not just Go/web code. Adds three features:
  - **Exact-IP allow-exceptions**: new `allowed_v4`/`allowed_v6` BPF hash maps (4096 entries each), checked in `decide4()`/`decide6()` right after the `enforcing()`/`scope_allows()` gate — an allow hit returns immediately, before any deny/CIDR/port/rate check runs. New `internal/store/allow.go` (`AddAllowed`/`DelAllowed`/`AddAllowedIPv6`/`DelAllowedIPv6`, mirroring the existing `AddBlocked`/`DelBlocked` pattern), `POST /api/v1/ebpf/allow` / `DELETE /api/v1/ebpf/allow/{ip}` API routes, `netractl ebpf allow add|del`, two new `netra-mcp` mutating tools (`netra_ebpf_allow_add`/`_delete`, bringing the mutating tool count to 40), and AI draft-rule support (`"allow 10.0.0.5"` now drafts an allow-exception instead of being rejected as unrecognized). The existing Firewall dashboard renders the new `allow4`/`allow6` rule types with no `web/` changes needed — its rules table is already generically driven by `r.type`/`r.value`.
  - **IPv6 destination PPS rate limiting**: new `rate_v6`/`rate_state_v6` maps giving `decide6()` parity with `decide4()`'s existing IPv4 `rate_v4`/`rate_state_v4` PPS ceiling. Agent's `replaceRates()` was rewritten to dispatch each configured destination to the v4 or v6 map based on `net.ParseIP(...).To4() != nil`, with a nil-check on the v6 map for backward compatibility during a rolling upgrade against an older BPF object.
  - **ICMP type histogram**: new observe-only `icmp_type_stats` map (256 entries), populated by a new `bump_icmp_type()` call added to the protocol dispatch's ICMP branch, bounds-checked before the single-byte dereference.
  - **Found and fixed a real bug in the kit's own `bpf/netra_tc.c`**: `decide6()` had no `apply_rate` parameter, so IPv6 rate limiting would have applied unconditionally at every call site — including the socket-sendmsg and XDP-ingress hooks, where `decide4()`'s existing calls explicitly pass `apply_rate=0` to skip rate limiting there. This directly contradicted the kit's own stated goal of IPv4/IPv6 parity. Fixed by adding `apply_rate` to `decide6()`'s signature (matching `decide4`'s parameter order) and gating the new `rate_limited6()` call with it, then updating all four call sites to pass the same values as their `decide4` counterparts (1, 1, 0, 0 for the skb-egress, skb-ingress, socket-sendmsg, and XDP-ingress hooks respectively). No pinned map was resized or had its ABI changed.
  - Same recurring pattern as every kit this session: the kit's raw `cmd/netractl/main.go` diff would have deleted the existing `case "explain":` command — rejected, merged by hand to keep both.
  - **Also fixed an unrelated pre-existing deadlock** found while running the full test suite for this merge: `internal/webhook/dispatcher_test.go`'s `TestPublishReturnsFalseWhenQueueFull` deferred `close(block)` *before* `srv.Close()`/`d.Stop()`, so on cleanup the server's `Close()` (and the dispatcher's `Stop()`) would block forever waiting for the one in-flight request to finish — but that request's handler was waiting on `block`, which only closed afterward. Moved `close(block)` to run explicitly before the deferred cleanup, right after the test's assertion loop. Confirmed via `git log`/`git diff` this was untouched by the eBPF kit or any other change this session — a real, previously-latent 10-minute test hang.
  - Local BPF compile isn't possible on this Mac (no Linux kernel headers, no podman/docker installed) — verified via `command diff -u` against the kit that the merged `bpf/netra_tc.c` matches it exactly except the `apply_rate` fix (5 hunks, all accounted for); real kernel verifier validation happens on next deploy to a live host with `NETRA_AGENT_ENABLED=true`.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (all green after the webhook fix), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (56/56)/build.
- Version bumped to 0.27.15 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.14 — 2026-09-13

Found live on the deployed dashboard, not in a kit. Changed the cap to match `main`'s own `max-width: 1800px` and padding scale, so the nav's content width now tracks the page content's actual width instead of an unrelated, much narrower one — brand pins to the true left edge, actions to the true right edge, and the already-tight 15-item nav-links row gets meaningfully more breathing room before its own horizontal scroll kicks in. Verified live via Chrome (style injected and previewed against the real deployment before rebuilding) and confirmed the existing `@media (max-width: 900px)` mobile nav rules are unaffected (they already override padding explicitly at that breakpoint).

## 0.27.10 — 2026-09-13

- **Extended the AI layer again** (0.27.9) with a third external PR kit continuing the same feature — same recurring pattern (full loose-file snapshot, `cmd/netractl/main.go` would have deleted `explain` if applied as given; merged by hand) but only two real deltas this time, since almost everything else in the kit already matched what was already merged:
  - **Wired the AI on-call digest into the existing webhook alert poller** (`internal/alert/poller.go`): each poll tick now also builds the digest (`internal/ai.BuildDigest`) and, if the cluster isn't quiet (severity above info, or the incident fingerprint changed since the last one seen), emits a `source=ai kind=digest` `webhook.Event` through the exact same dedup/cooldown/dispatch path as every other alert source — no new thresholds, no new detector, just a narrative rollup over the same counters. `internal/webhook.Event` gained two optional fields (`fingerprint`, `card`) that stay empty on every existing event type. Documented a real architectural caveat this introduces: the digest's "last fingerprint" is in-process, unpersisted, **global** state shared with the interactive `GET /api/v1/ai/digest` endpoint — running the poller and querying that endpoint manually can each affect the other's `changed` flag, since there's one "last seen" slot, not one per caller.
  - **Added per-finding "Explain" buttons** (`web/src/components/ExplainFinding.tsx`, new) to the Health and Drops pages' anomaly/finding rows — a small popover that calls `POST /api/v1/ai/explain` for that one row and shows the narrated severity/headline/summary/next-steps, reusing existing `.severity-badge`/`.warning`/`.btn-secondary` styling plus two new CSS rules.
  - Rejected one hunk from the kit's own `internal/mcpserver/mcpserver_test.go`: it would have reverted the escaped-substring assertion fix from 0.27.9 (the kit's branch predates that fix, so its own copy of the test still has the original bug) — kept the corrected version.
  - The kit's own `docs/ai.md`/`docs/mcp-integration.md` still didn't include the Resources section or the four `digest`/`draft`/`explain`/`suggestions`-related additions this project's docs already carry from 0.27.9 — left those as-is rather than reverting to the kit's less-complete copies. `docs/alerting.md` gained the digest-event documentation and the global-state caveat above; `README.md`'s alerting and AI bullets were extended to mention both.
  - Verified: `go build/vet/test ./...` (including a new poller test confirming the digest event fires on critical health and correctly dedupes within cooldown while the fingerprint is unchanged), `helm lint`, web typecheck/test (51/51)/build. Along the way, `go test ./...` surfaced a genuinely pre-existing, unrelated flaky test (`internal/webhook.TestPublishReturnsFalseWhenQueueFull`, last touched in the original 0.20.0 alerting commit) — reproduced it hanging once and passing twice with zero code changes between runs, confirming it predates and is independent of this merge; left as a known flake rather than attempting an unscoped fix.
- Version bumped to 0.27.10 across all six tracked locations; `web/package-lock.json` regenerated.

## 0.27.9 — 2026-09-13

- **Extended the AI layer** (0.27.8) with a second external PR kit continuing the same feature — again a full loose-file snapshot rather than a patch, and again individually diffed or read against this repo's real current code before merging (its own `cmd/netractl/main.go` would, once more, have silently deleted the `explain` command had it been applied as given; merged by hand to keep both):

  - `netractl ai` gains `digest`, `suggestions`, `draft QUESTION...`, and `explain KIND [MESSAGE...]` alongside the existing `status`/`brief`/`ask`.
  - `netra-mcp` gains a fourth MCP capability, **resources** (`resources/list`/`resources/read`, `internal/mcpserver`), four read-only resources (`netra://ai/brief`, `netra://ai/digest`, `netra://ai/suggestions`, `netra://status`), four new tools (`netra_ai_draft`, `netra_ai_digest`, `netra_ai_suggestions`, `netra_ai_explain`), and two new prompts (`netra_draft_rule`, `netra_oncall_digest`). Fixed a genuine bug in the kit's own shipped test (`TestResourcesListAndRead`): it asserted on the unescaped substring `"ok":true`, which can never match since that text lives inside a JSON string field in the actual response and is therefore always escaped (`\"ok\":true`) — the feature itself was already correct, only the test assertion was wrong.

Loads the brief on mount, posts questions from a text field or live suggestion chips, shows a rule-draft preview when a question looks like a deny/rate request (never an Apply button), and a "copy on-call card" action surfacing the incident fingerprint. Built entirely from this app's existing design tokens/classes (`.card`, `.severity-badge`, `.toolbar`, `.primary`, `.btn-secondary`, `.empty-state`) plus a small set of new `.ask-netra-*`/`.ask-chip` rules appended to `styles.css` with zero overlap with the black-theme system from 0.27.7 — verified visually in both dark and light mode.
